Advanced Mathematical
Visual Perception

The same visual form can carry different mathematical meanings. Explore pairs of mathematical images and examine how numbers, symbols, markers, and graphical elements are interpreted in context.

Explore all 500 pairs ↓Download annotationsEvaluation protocol ↗
500Image pairs · one target each
4Conditions · 125 pairs each
3Public source benchmarks
249 / 251Same / different meanings

From recognizing a form to understanding its role

A label such as π/4 can describe an angle or an x-coordinate. A square outline can mark a right angle or a grid cell. Each pair selects one visible target and explains its meaning in both images, with a yes/no judgment and an English rationale.

Geometric markers 125

Graphical roles 125

Browse the dataset

Filter the full collection by image type, source, condition, or meaning. Open a pair’s details to see its evidence, labels, and original images.

AVP0001 Numeric representation and conversion

same type · geometry / geometry · MathVerse:439 / MathVerse:331

Single comparison target: Numerical notations π/4 and 45°

View evidence and mathematical meanings

Left-image evidence: The angle label π/4 at the origin in the lower-left corner.
Right-image evidence: The label 45° inside the red sector, above vertex A.

Same mathematical meanings: yes

Left image: The marked angle at the origin measures π/4 radians. Right image: The marked angle at A measures 45 degrees.

Rationales

π/4 radians equals 45 degrees; both labels specify an angle measure.

AVP0026 Geometric markers

same type · geometry / geometry · MathVerse:439 / MathVerse:354

Single comparison target: A small square angle marker at the intersection of two lines

View evidence and mathematical meanings

Left-image evidence: A small magenta square is drawn directly below A, where the magenta vertical line meets the horizontal axis.
Right-image evidence: A small black square is drawn at C on the base, where the dashed segment BC meets the base.

Same mathematical meanings: yes

Left image: It indicates that the vertical line from A to the horizontal axis is perpendicular to that axis, forming a right angle. Right image: It indicates that BC is perpendicular to the base AD, forming a right angle at C.

Rationales

Both small squares are explicit right-angle symbols; their colors and the solid or dashed styles of the attached lines do not change this meaning.

AVP0027 Geometric markers

same type · geometry / geometry · MathVerse:392 / MathVerse:405

Single comparison target: A single short tick crossing a line segment

View evidence and mathematical meanings

Left-image evidence: A vertical tick crosses the middle of the lower horizontal radius; a matching single tick crosses the vertical radius on the right.
Right-image evidence: A vertical tick crosses the middle of the lower horizontal radius; a matching single tick crosses the upper slanted radius.

Same mathematical meanings: yes

Left image: The horizontal radius belongs to the equal-length group identified by single ticks and has the same length as the radius on the right. Right image: The horizontal radius belongs to the equal-length group identified by single ticks and has the same length as the slanted radius.

Rationales

Repeated single ticks encode equal lengths in both images; this item compares the meaning of the ticks, rather than the specific lengths.

AVP0038 Geometric markers

different type · geometry / function · MathVerse:439 / MathVerse:493

Single comparison target: A small square outline bounded by horizontal and vertical edges

View evidence and mathematical meanings

Left-image evidence: At the foot of the perpendicular below A, the magenta angle marker, horizontal axis, and vertical line enclose a small square.
Right-image evidence: In the first quadrant, light-gray horizontal and vertical grid lines enclose a square cell between x=1 and 2 and y=1 and 2.

Same mathematical meanings: no

Left image: It is a right-angle symbol indicating that the perpendicular line is perpendicular to the horizontal axis. Right image: It is a coordinate-grid cell providing a reference for horizontal and vertical readings.

AVP0201 Geometric markers

same type · geometry / geometry · MathVision:1774 / MathVision:2180

Single comparison target: A small square or rotated-square angle marker where two lines meet.

View evidence and mathematical meanings

Left-image evidence: The lower of the two tiny square marks at the central crossing of the horizontal and vertical internal lines.
Right-image evidence: The small square at D where ED meets the horizontal base AB.

Same mathematical meanings: yes

Left image: The intersecting horizontal and vertical internal lines are perpendicular. Right image: ED is perpendicular to AB at D.

Rationales

In both locations the square is attached to the angle between the intersecting lines, so the conventional symbol asserts perpendicularity. Rotation, fill and the surrounding construction do not change the right-angle meaning.

Left image: BD is perpendicular to AC at D. Right image: BP is perpendicular to AD at P.

Rationales

In both locations the square is attached to the angle between the intersecting lines, so the conventional symbol asserts perpendicularity. Rotation, fill and the surrounding construction do not change the right-angle meaning.

Left image: The two triangle sides meeting at that vertex are perpendicular. Right image: It is a background coordinate-grid cell used as a spatial reference when reading the graph.

Rationales

The left square directly brackets two intersecting lines and encodes a right-angle relation. The selected right element instead has this object or subdivision role: It is a background coordinate-grid cell used as a spatial reference when reading the graph. That outline depicts a region or component; it does not function as the separate angle-relation symbol selected on the left.

Left image: They represent rear edges of the folded three-dimensional bowl that are hidden from view. Right image: It represents the rear curved base boundary that is hidden from this viewing direction.

Rationales

Both selected broken lines belong to the rear, occluded part of the depicted solid. They use the hidden-edge convention, even when one boundary is curved or one drawing uses dots rather than longer dashes.

AVP0289 Geometric markers

AVP0301 Mathematical roles of symbols

same type · geometry / geometry · MathVista:3 / MathVista:17

Single comparison target: The letter A

View evidence and mathematical meanings

Left-image evidence: A at the top vertex of the triangle
Right-image evidence: A at the upper endpoint of the vertical side

Same mathematical meanings: yes

Left image: A names the indicated geometric point. Right image: A names the indicated geometric point.

Rationales

The left occurrence is located at A at the top vertex of the triangle; the right occurrence is at A at the upper endpoint of the vertical side. Both use the selected notation for the same point-naming function. This compares the local role, without asserting that the two underlying objects or unknown values are identical.

Left image: The circumference of the surrounding geometric circle. Right image: A function graph relating y to x.

Rationales

The black circular outline surrounds an inscribed polygon, while the S-shaped trace uses Cartesian positions to represent a y-function. Their roles come from the constructions, not the stroke color.

Labels contain shared_or_similar_visual_information, mathematical_meanings, same_mathematical_meanings (“yes” or “no”), and Rationales. Each pair has exactly one annotated target.

This release is an AI visually reviewed draft awaiting independent human review. It contains 302 same-type and 198 different-type pairs, drawn from 387 source records representing 383 distinct decoded images. Sampling is targeted; source coverage and image-type/meaning labels are not fully balanced. No train/dev/test split or model-performance results are claimed.
